Most days I wake up with this heavy, flat sadness in my chest, like there’s no point in trying to build the life I was planning.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: and my clothes have gotten looser.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely appointment screen and my brain just blanks, so I second-guess simple things I used to do automatically.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don’t have a plan to hurt myself, but I get thoughts like it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up and nobody had to deal with me anymore.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When the sadness got heavy and stayed, the voice at the shampoo stations and the belief that someone was sabotaging my bookings were happening at the same time, not separately.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: Looking back, I have spent most of this whole decline feeling depressed and weighed down, with only shorter stretches where the sadness was not as prominent.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any stiffness in your muscles?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’d call it stiffness, no. I mean, I get tense sometimes, like anyone, especially if I’m standing all day, but I haven’t really noticed my muscles feeling rigid or locked up. At least not that I can think of. Is that okay—like, is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to sound dramatic, but… most days I wake up and there’s this kind of heavy, flat sadness in my chest. Like before I even get out of bed, it’s already there.\n\nIt just feels like… what’s the point of trying so hard to build the life I thought I was going to have, you know? I’m still trying, I am, but it’s been hard to shake that feeling. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. I mean, I still do things if I have to, like at work, but it doesn’t feel the same.\n\nI used to really like chatting with clients and helping the newer stylists, and I’d go hiking with friends when I could. Lately I kind of just… keep my head down and get through the day. I avoid people more than I should, probably. It’s not that I’m trying to be rude or anything, I just don’t really have that spark in me right now.\n\nIs that what you mean by losing interest?\n\nDoctor: Have these low mood feelings been present nearly every day for at least 2 weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s definitely been more than two weeks. I kind of wish it wasn’t, but… the heavy depression feeling has been there for about a year and two months now.\n\nNot every second, I guess, but most days it’s there in some way. Is that the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, had you ever gone through another period in your life when you felt depressed like this?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. Not really like this, I don’t think.\n\nI mean, I’ve had sad times before, like normal stuff, but I don’t remember it being this heavy or lasting this long. At least… nothing that stands out to me the way this past year has.\n\nIs that an okay answer?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your walking has become slower?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little… I don’t know if it’s my walking specifically, but I’ve definitely gotten slower doing things.\n\nAt work, my hands feel like they’re moving through glue sometimes. Even simple stuff like sectioning hair or mixing color takes me way longer than it used to. I try to hide it and just keep going, but people have started noticing I’m slower, I think.\n\nIs that kind of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. It just takes more effort than I’d like to admit, I guess. Is that the kind of tired you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually sensitive to cold lately?\n\nPatient: Um… not that I’ve really noticed, no.\n\nI mean, I get cold sometimes like anyone, especially if the salon’s air is on, but I don’t think it’s been unusual or anything. I haven’t really been paying close attention to that, though. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping much more or much less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I don’t think it’s been, like, dramatically different.\n\nSome nights I sleep more, some nights less, but I haven’t been keeping track or anything. I’m still trying, I am, but it’s been hard to shake that feeling. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. I mean, I still do things if I have to, like at work, but it doesn’t feel the same.\n\nI used to really like chatting with clients and helping the newer stylists, and I’d go hiking with friends when I could. Lately I kind of just… keep my head down and get through the day. I avoid people more than I should, probably. It’s not that I’m trying to be rude or anything, I just don’t really have that spark in me right now.\n\nIs that what you mean by losing interest?\n\nDoctor: Have these low mood feelings been present nearly every day for at least 2 weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s definitely been more than two weeks. I kind of wish it wasn’t, but… the heavy depression feeling has been there for about a year and two months now.\n\nNot every second, I guess, but most days it’s there in some way. Is that the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, had you ever gone through another period in your life when you felt depressed like this?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any stiffness in your muscles?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’d call it stiffness, no. I mean, I get tense sometimes, like anyone, especially if I’m standing all day, but I haven’t really noticed my muscles feeling rigid or locked up. At least not that I can think of. Is that okay—like, is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to sound dramatic, but… most days I wake up and there’s this kind of heavy, flat sadness in my chest. Like before I even get out of bed, it’s already there.\n\nIt just feels like… what’s the point of trying so hard to build the life I thought I was going to have, you know? I’m still trying, I am, but it’s been hard to shake that feeling. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. I mean, I still do things if I have to, like at work, but it doesn’t feel the same.\n\nI used to really like chatting with clients and helping the newer stylists, and I’d go hiking with friends when I could. Lately I kind of just… keep my head down and get through the day. I avoid people more than I should, probably. It’s not that I’m trying to be rude or anything, I just don’t really have that spark in me right now.\n\nIs that what you mean by losing interest?\n\nDoctor: Have these low mood feelings been present nearly every day for at least 2 weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s definitely been more than two weeks. I kind of wish it wasn’t, but… the heavy depression feeling has been there for about a year and two months now.\n\nNot every second, I guess, but most days it’s there in some way. Is that the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, had you ever gone through another period in your life when you felt depressed like this?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. Not really like this, I don’t think.\n\nI mean, I’ve had sad times before, like normal stuff, but I don’t remember it being this heavy or lasting this long. At least… nothing that stands out to me the way this past year has.\n\nIs that an okay answer?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your walking has become slower?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little… I don’t know if it’s my walking specifically, but I’ve definitely gotten slower doing things.\n\nAt work, my hands feel like they’re moving through glue sometimes. Even simple stuff like sectioning hair or mixing color takes me way longer than it used to. I try to hide it and just keep going, but people have started noticing I’m slower, I think.\n\nIs that kind of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I mean, I do feel kind of drained a lot, but I don’t know if I’d call it “unusually” tired or if it’s just… life, you know?\n\nI can still get through work and stuff, mostly. It just takes more effort than I’d like to admit, I guess. Is that the kind of tired you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually sensitive to cold lately?\n\nPatient: Um… not that I’ve really noticed, no.\n\nI mean, I get cold sometimes like anyone, especially if the salon’s air is on, but I don’t think it’s been unusual or anything. I haven’t really been paying close attention to that, though. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping much more or much less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I don’t think it’s been, like, dramatically different.\n\nSome nights I sleep more, some nights less, but I haven’t been keeping track or anything. I guess I’d say it’s been kind of normal for me? Unless there’s a certain amount you mean that I should be noticing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your body and thoughts have been moving noticeably slower than usual?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, I guess maybe a little. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but at work my hands feel like they’re moving through glue sometimes.\n\nLike, sectioning hair or mixing color just takes me way longer than it used to. People have kind of noticed too, that I’m slower, which is… embarrassing. Is that what you mean by moving slower?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I think. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but at work I’ll look at a color formula or the appointment screen and my mind just kind of… blanks.\n\nThen I start second-guessing really simple stuff I used to do without thinking. Like I know I know it, but in the moment it takes me longer to get there. Does that count as trouble concentrating?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your handwriting getting smaller than it used to be?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed my handwriting specifically getting smaller, no. I don’t write by hand a ton.\n\nBut with my hands in general… yeah, things feel slower. Like at work, sectioning hair or mixing color feels like my hands are moving through glue, and it takes me forever compared to before. People have noticed I’m slower too, which is kind of embarrassing. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting short of breath more easily than usual?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not that I’ve really noticed.\n\nI mean, I get tired sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’m getting out of breath more easily or anything like that. Is there something specific I should be looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that your face looks less expressive than usual?\n\nPatient: Um… maybe. No one has said it exactly like that, I don’t think.\n\nBut people have asked if I’m okay, or said I seem kind of… flat, I guess. I used to be really chatty with clients and help the newer stylists, and after work I’d go hiking with friends. Lately I mostly just keep my head down and try not to talk too much. It’s not that I’m trying to be rude or anything—I just don’t really have that spark in me right now.\n\nDoes that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you walk, has anyone noticed that one of your arms doesn’t swing as much as it used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure about the arm-swing thing specifically… no one’s pointed that out to me.\n\nBut I guess the closest thing is just that I’ve gotten slower with my hands. At work, sectioning hair or mixing color feels like my hands are moving through glue, and it takes me way longer than it used to. A couple people have noticed I’m slower, which is… kind of embarrassing. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any stiffness in your muscles?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’d call it stiffness, no. Is that okay—like, is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to sound dramatic, but… most days I wake up and there’s this kind of heavy, flat sadness in my chest. I’m still trying, I am, but it’s been hard to shake that feeling. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. I mean, I still do things if I have to, like at work, but it doesn’t feel the same.\n\nI used to really like chatting with clients and helping the newer stylists, and I’d go hiking with friends when I could. Lately I kind of just… keep my head down and get through the day. I avoid people more than I should, probably. It’s not that I’m trying to be rude or anything, I just don’t really have that spark in me right now.\n\nIs that what you mean by losing interest?\n\nDoctor: Have these low mood feelings been present nearly every day for at least 2 weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s definitely been more than two weeks. I kind of wish it wasn’t, but… the heavy depression feeling has been there for about a year and two months now.\n\nNot every second, I guess, but most days it’s there in some way. Is that the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: Before this past year, had you ever gone through another period in your life when you felt depressed like this?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. Not really like this, I don’t think.\n\nI mean, I’ve had sad times before, like normal stuff, but I don’t remember it being this heavy or lasting this long. At least… nothing that stands out to me the way this past year has.\n\nIs that an okay answer?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your walking has become slower?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little… I don’t know if it’s my walking specifically, but I’ve definitely gotten slower doing things.\n\nAt work, my hands feel like they’re moving through glue sometimes. Even simple stuff like sectioning hair or mixing color takes me way longer than it used to. I try to hide it and just keep going, but people have started noticing I’m slower, I think.\n\nIs that kind of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I mean, I do feel kind of drained a lot, but I don’t know if I’d call it “unusually” tired or if it’s just… life, you know?\n\nI can still get through work and stuff, mostly. It just takes more effort than I’d like to admit, I guess. Is that the kind of tired you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually sensitive to cold lately?\n\nPatient: Um… not that I’ve really noticed, no.\n\nI mean, I get cold sometimes like anyone, especially if the salon’s air is on, but I don’t think it’s been unusual or anything. I haven’t really been paying close attention to that, though. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping much more or much less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I don’t think it’s been, like, dramatically different.\n\nSome nights I sleep more, some nights less, but I haven’t been keeping track or anything. I guess I’d say it’s been kind of normal for me? Unless there’s a certain amount you mean that I should be noticing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your body and thoughts have been moving noticeably slower than usual?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, I guess maybe a little. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but at work my hands feel like they’re moving through glue sometimes.\n\nLike, sectioning hair or mixing color just takes me way longer than it used to. People have kind of noticed too, that I’m slower, which is… embarrassing. Is that what you mean by moving slower?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I think. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but at work I’ll look at a color formula or the appointment screen and my mind just kind of… blanks.\n\nThen I start second-guessing really simple stuff I used to do without thinking. Like I know I know it, but in the moment it takes me longer to get there. Does that count as trouble concentrating?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your handwriting getting smaller than it used to be?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed my handwriting specifically getting smaller, no. I don’t write by hand a ton.\n\nBut with my hands in general… yeah, things feel slower. Like at work, sectioning hair or mixing color feels like my hands are moving through glue, and it takes me forever compared to before. People have noticed I’m slower too, which is kind of embarrassing. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting short of breath more easily than usual?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not that I’ve really noticed.\n\nI mean, I get tired sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’m getting out of breath more easily or anything like that. Is there something specific I should be looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that your face looks less expressive than usual?\n\nPatient: Um… maybe. No one has said it exactly like that, I don’t think.\n\nBut people have asked if I’m okay, or said I seem kind of… flat, I guess. I used to be really chatty with clients and help the newer stylists, and after work I’d go hiking with friends. Lately I mostly just keep my head down and try not to talk too much. It’s not that I’m trying to be rude or anything—I just don’t really have that spark in me right now.\n\nDoes that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you walk, has anyone noticed that one of your arms doesn’t swing as much as it used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure about the arm-swing thing specifically… no one’s pointed that out to me.\n\nBut I guess the closest thing is just that I’ve gotten slower with my hands. A couple people have noticed I’m slower, which is… kind of embarrassing. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any tremor or shaking in one hand or arm when you’re not using it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like when I’m just sitting there and my hand starts shaking on its own.\n\nSometimes my hands feel a little unsteady if I’m nervous or if I’ve had too much coffee, but I haven’t noticed a tremor in one hand or arm at rest. A little, I think… I wasn’t really trying to lose weight or anything.
